SANAYI, TICARET, ENERJI, TABI EVIL LOCATIONS, INFORMATION AND TEKNOLOGY COMMINATION PRESIDENT ZIYA ALTUNYALDIZ (KUNIA) Mr. President, valued lawmakers, I'm going to brief you on the overall and details of our proposal to the nuclear regulatory agency. Our General Assembly has established basic principles for the implementation of activities related to nuclear energy and ionizing radiation, as we have begun to discuss with them today. In the setting of these principles, dear friends, international regulations such as the European Union's 2013/59 Euratom Ionizing Standards for Basic Protection Against the Dangers caused by Radiation, the Convention on Nuclear Security, the Agreement on Dispersion of Nuclear Weapons, and the Convention on Computing Nuclear Trials, have been widely evaluated, analyzed, and taken into consideration. The proposal was specifically defined by the International Atomic Energy Agency, where our country is a party, and the concepts of security in international terminology, security, and nuclear security are also defined in detail. On the other hand, dear friends, the 2004 Protocol, which changed the 1960 Paris Convention, which we have been on since 1961, has taken effect in our country and in the world as of January 2022. In some cases, the Paris Convention, which has been significantly altered by the 2004 Protocol, which has been passed in accordance with the law and has been adopted in our Supreme Assembly, gives the states on which it is part of the internal law a certain obligation to regulate it, and in some issues it also gives them the authority to appreciate it. These obligations and discretionary powers have been detailed by our institutions, and the law proposal, which will be discussed today in our General Assembly, is also provided in the proposed law, which has begun to be discussed and its materials, and also in the detailed arrangement of the obligations given to entity states by the 2004 Protocol, which have been provided by the department of Legal Responsibility for Nuclear Damages. The Proposal of the Nuclear Editing Act determines the duties, authority and responsibilities of the Nuclear Organization, which has the authority to regulate, authorise, audit and sanction the field in order to ensure the peaceful and secure production of nuclear energy and to establish regulatory control over ionizing radiation activities. Dear friends, I expressed it in my speech yesterday. The story of our country, which originally began with nuclear, has come to this day, and as of today, the Nuclear Organization will have provided the opportunity, in particular, to bring to our country different abilities, such as authoritization, regulation, insurance and safety, and the key work of technology, particularly human resources, to make our country the owner of our country in a sense. Moreover, this offer, my dear friends, as I have just mentioned, will be given to our nation, our country, as I hope, as an important provision for our country, which regulates very important issues on the level of law, such as the principle and rules on nuclear energy and radioactive waste. To discuss this offer, dear friends, I'd like to mention a few things in particular. What do we mean, no authorization? The proposal has ruled that activities related to nuclear energy and ionizing radiation cannot be carried out without notification of protection or without authority from the Institution. In this scope, the institution has set up a license and a license for the operation of the subject activities, as well as details of issues that will be subject to the institution's approval of nuclear facilities, radiation facilities and the establishment of radioactive waste. I wanted to explain to you, our esteemed deputies, in a more detailed way, the scope of authorization that we have mentioned in our talks. What do we mean by inspection, dear friends? The audit has also drawn a framework of audit and study activities that will be conducted by the Institution. The authority and responsibilities of those assigned or employed by the institution to accompany or conduct audits are determined by the Institution, and its control of structures in the areas of licensed nuclear facilities and radioactive waste facilities will be carried out by the Institution. The proposal also concerned that the actual responsibility for the security of the nuclear facility, which was held in Article 9 of the Nuclear Security Convention, was authorized by the person, and also arranged the issue by the law. In particular, I would like to brief you on the radioactive waste that our fellow speakers bring up during the general talks. Dear friends, you are aware that the proposal has included provisions regarding radioactive waste and used fuels. Radioactive waste, which emerged during an activity outside the sovereign domain of the Republic of Turkey, has been banned from entering within the borders of the Republic of Turkey, so it has been ruled that all responsibility belongs to the person authorized to operate under the administration of radioactive waste, except for the emerging used fuels or disposal. Turkey's Energy, Nuclear and Mining Research Agency has been authorized to dispose of radioactive waste due to activities in the area of sovereignty of the Republic of Turkey. From time to time, our friends have also expressed concerns about how legal responsibilities are being organised and what the framework is about nuclear fields or facilities to be built. And I'd like to make a brief statement about that, dear friends. The proposal also included the agreement on legal responsibility in the nuclear energy field, which we are part of, in parallel with the changes to the Paris Convention, which came with the 2004 Protocol, the regime of legal responsibility in the field of nuclear energy. The damage caused by the nuclear plant's nuclear plant or the removal of nuclear materials, regardless of whether it has any flaws in its staff, its staff and its suppliers of goods and services -- I want to underline this particular issue -- is subject to a very clear arrangement of the property and service provider, regardless of whether or not the personnel have any flaws in the nuclear field, and in the specific amount of time and time that they will be responsible. It is mandatory that the responsibility be secured with insurance or other financial guarantees, and the issue has been made too clear for doubt and interpretation. In writing the provisions of the law, the Paris Convention, which is under an internal law in our country, has been completely loyal to norms and principles under the rule of legal responsibility established by the 2004 Protocol, and the Paris Convention has also been arranged. tbmm-2022-03-03sit04spe0183par0088	Thank you, Mr. President. I have made a pledge on behalf of the National Party of Action on the first chapter of the 314th Amendment. Nuclear energy was on the agenda of politicians, scientists and industrialists, which began in 1879 with the discovery of uranium and continued in 1934 with the disintegration of the atom. As with many other technological developments, studies that began in the field of military defence later continued commercially. Many countries, including the United States and Russia, have done intensive work on the use of nuclear energy, and have developed systems that will convert the heat energy that is revealed as a result of the break-up of atoms into electrical energy. The expansion of nuclear plants began in the early 1970s with the oil crisis. Without oil and other hydrocarbon sources, countries have turned to the nuclear power plant to ensure their dependence on these resources and their energy supply security. While nuclear power plants were quickly being put into business around the world, it continued to be built around the world in 1979, even though there was a relatively slowdown in the Three Mile Island and Soviet Russia in 1986. After Turkey's approval of the Paris Climate Agreement and in 2053 it should be seen as an essential element of nuclear energy in line with the goal of having a carbon neutral economy and energy system. Therefore, nuclear must be included in Turkey's energy mixture. All low-carbon energy technologies, including nuclear energy, are needed to reach the goal of limiting the Paris Agreement's global temperature increase to less than two degrees. According to a report by the European Economic Council of the United Nations, nuclear energy will be a source of energy that will help meet the Paris Agreement and its 2030 Sustainable Development Goals. As of July 2018, 453 nuclear reactors operate in 31 countries and 57 nuclear reactors are being built in 17 countries. Electricity produced in nuclear power plants corresponds to 11 per cent of the world's electricity supply. According to the country, about 72 per cent of the French electricity demand, 55 per cent of Ukraine, Belgium, Sweden 40 per cent, South Korea 27 per cent, the European Union 30 per cent, and the United States 20 per cent are covered by nuclear energy. 15 of the nuclear reactors under construction are in China, 7 are in India, 6 are in Russia. However, in the United States, 2, 4 in the United Arab Emirates, 4 in South Korea, 4 in France and Turkey are building nuclear reactors. Our country's aim to build a half-century nuclear power plant began with the signing of the Agreement on 12 May 2010 of a nuclear power plant in Akkuyu Field in the Republic of Turkey between the Republic of Turkey and the Russian Federation government. In addition to Akkuyu, work continues for two new nuclear plants in the region of Sinop and Thrace. To ensure economic stability and continuity, you must ensure the continuity of the energy supply. Despite the limited resources of the world, the energy demand is very high and is increasing day by day. In today's conditions, most of the energy needs are still derived from fossil fuels. It is estimated that fossil fuel resources will be exhausted within 50 years as a result of a simple projection of energy demand maintaining the current rate of increase. For Turkey, which has to import most of its energy needs, it must add nuclear plants to its energy supply portfolio to keep its development move at a sustainable level and to increase its industry to levels of international competition. With an increasing demand for energy due to economic development, requests for new and renewable energy from alternative energy sources and nuclear energy are seen to rise, depending on research that coal from fossil fuels will be exhausted in two hundred and fifty years, 50 years. Turkey's use of nuclear energy as an alternative energy form in energy dependence and energy supply security is increasing day by day. Although nuclear power plants have high cost, low fuel and operating costs are an important advantage. Nuclear energy as an alternative to natural gas, which is dependent on the world. The negative effects of nuclear plants, such as soil pollution and radiation, can be said to be relatively less than fossil fuels, such as oil, coal and natural gas, and more than renewable energy sources. Although localisation policies in the field have been increasing recently, they have not yet reached enough levels. Nuclear energy in energy production is an option that needs to be evaluated in countries such as Turkey, which are in an out-dependent position on oil and natural gas, which have recently begun to accelerate work on underground resources. The money Turkey spends on energy imports is increasingly challenging the state's industry in the international arena. The emphasis on the need for nuclear energy production is not only for the satisfaction of consumption, but also for Turkey's strategic plans to become a great state, as well as for its advantage. Energy production through nuclear power plants provides continuity in energy supply, while the production stage contains a series of non-failable processes. The scenarios that could have been caused by an accident must be carefully evaluated, and the public health decision-making authorities must always make the necessary moves in the first place. The global energy system faces two important challenges today. The first is more energy production, and the second is less carbon emissions. More explicitly, studies continue to use production technologies that are least harmful to the environment as cheap and sustainable energy supplies are intended. In this context, the use of nuclear energy provides significant advantages, even if it contains some risks. Some of these advantages can be listed as continuous and uninterrupted production of electricity using nuclear power, producing electricity in nuclear plants at more affordable costs than other plants, lack of greenhouse gas emissions, and the production of energy of nuclear technology, as well as the use of physics, medicine, transport, agriculture in many areas. Nuclear energy has a number of negativeities as well as the advantages mentioned here. The most important topic in this area is nuclear waste and their storage processes. While nuclear energy is being obtained, there is a large amount of radioactive waste, and the preservation of these wastes brings with it a serious risk element. In our country, the study of nuclear energy is based on 1956. We were the founding member of the International Atomic Energy Agency this year, but by 1957 we formed the Atomic Energy Commission. In the 1960s, the Atomic Energy Commission carried out many important works, including the establishment of our research reactors, the selection of areas for our nuclear plants, including Akkuyu and Sinop, and the creation of our education infrastructure for the creation of human resources. By 1982, the Atomic Energy Commission was restructured to ensure the development of our nuclear energy infrastructure and to build nuclear power plants, and Turkey's Atomic Energy Agency, the TAEK, was established. From 1982 to 2018, TAEK continued its activities as an institution that conducts both the RD in nuclear energy, conducts radioactive waste management and operates our research reactors and regulates and supervises nuclear energy and condenser radiation. By the year 2018, it became necessary to introduce internationally acceptable nuclear security standards to our administrative structure, to meet our obligations in international agreements, and to make a new regulation in the field of nuclear energy to move the legal framework in our country one step further. In this context, studies have been launched to separate TAEK's regulatory, auditory function from its operating structure in the field of nuclear energy and radiation applications, and the Institution has been established by 2/7/2018 dates and 702. Radioactive waste management, RD activities and other radiation activities remained within TAEK, and a series of subsequent regulations have been restructured under the name TAEK, Turkey's Energy, Nuclear and Mining Research Agency, briefly called ENTENMAK. The proposal also envisioned the establishment of the Nuclear Technical Support Corporation (NUTED) in order to provide technical support services such as analysis, counselling, monitoring, research, examination, testing, training and certification, at least 51 per cent of the shares belong to the Nuclear Organization. See, as an exception, the article's third joke, which is produced within the borders of the Republic of Turkey, does not apply to radiation sources exported to the ground when it expires, the transit of radioactive waste, and import of materials that are infected with natural radioactive materials. What we understand from this expression is that, for example, you produced uranium in Turkey, you exported this uranium, another country used it, and then we'll take its waste back. As the law says, if the origin was exported back to the country, we'll take it back. This is a very dangerous situation. We're going to export uranium, but why do we accept its waste? Is that a condition you need to sell? Why are you putting our country in colonial status? How can you accept that? Look, in 2007, radioactive rods were detected in Izmir Gaziemir, which were used in 100 tons of nuclear reactors, buried in the field of the lead factory. Since we don't have nuclear reactors, we don't know where these nuclear rods came from, which should belong to a reactor overseas. According to a newspaper report, the amount of radiation here is 7,200 times greater than normal. Is it worth the risk? Can we be willing to allow such nuclear garbage to come out of various parts of our country in the future? Also, it's also a matter in Article 9 that must be removed from the proposal text, allowing radioactive waste to pass through, because the transit of radioactive waste is a great risk to public safety. Therefore, I respectfully salute the General Assembly, wishing that our priority is to use our renewable energy capacity and to prioritize technological investments in these matters.

tbmm-2022-03-03sit07spe0326par0029	Mr. President, the Court, the valued MPs, is actually a key item in the matter of nuclear energy, nuclear regulatory law. In 2010, a treaty between Russia and Russia's Federation of Russia had been established before it had been established, to be kidnapped from the Constitutional Court's control, and after the treaty was established, and the Constitutional Court had not been able to inspect it, and there was no way. In fact, the Constitutional Court is now making a legal arrangement on the Constitutional Court, which the Constitutional Court has cancelled, as it organises what the Council of Ministers should not organise, rather than what the Council of Ministers should not. Now, the main institution of this legal arrangement is the Nuclear Control Agency, but there is a missing arrangement here. This arrangement is not limited to the incomplete arrangement. There is a dangerous arrangement, and unless the status of this institution is determined, many violations will continue in chains. Because in Article 3, these Institutions are given duties and powers to oversee activities involving risk of exposure to radiation, regulate radiation doses, and oversee them; they are given the responsibility to inform the public on these matters, and they are authorized to issue regulations in this area. The powers vested in this institution can only be used by an autonomous and expert public institution. Because according to Article 124, the Constitution shall be issued by the President -- the Council of Ministers -- by the ministries and by the people of the public. Therefore, even though this contract and this particular clause, even though it is sending it to international contracts and the standards set by international contracts, and the fact that such institutions are not given such a status, even though it requires autonomy and expertise, makes this legal arrangement incomplete, incompatible with the Constitution and the Constitution, dangerous.
